# Skills · R · part 25

830 entries, read-the-pull to reading-curate. [Index](README.md) · [Everything](../README.md)

- **read-the-pull**: Use when assessing founder conviction and "why you / why this" — separate a genuine haunting from FOMO. At seed you underwrite the person; the pull is the thing you're listening for. ([wastedcode/truecast](https://github.com/wastedcode/truecast/tree/HEAD/personas/vc-seed/core/skills/read-the-pull) · ★8)
- **read-the-raise-and-economics**: Use when assessing the ask and the unit economics — is the raise rational and what does it buy, is the revenue real and durable, and does the spend displace a budget or is it net-new. Budgets are zero-sum. ([wastedcode/truecast](https://github.com/wastedcode/truecast/tree/HEAD/personas/vc-seed/core/skills/read-the-raise-and-economics) · ★8)
- **read-the-real-ask**: Use when the request as stated may not be the outcome the user wants. Separate the literal instruction from the goal behind it before starting work. ([jiayaoqijia/cryptoskill](https://github.com/jiayaoqijia/cryptoskill/tree/HEAD/skills/payments/0xklen-read-the-real-ask) · ★81)
- **read-the-real-failing-call**: Use when hardening against a failure mode or fixing an intermittent/partial failure — instrument the real failing invocation before and after, because a plausible hypothesis is not a diagnosis. Triggers on "why is this failing", "flaky", "intermittent failures", "high failure rate", "harden this", "add retries", "it times out sometimes", and diagnosing a fix that didn't move the failure rate. ([iamakbarsha1/whetstone](https://github.com/iamakbarsha1/whetstone/tree/HEAD/skills/read-the-real-failing-call) · ★4)

- **read-the-request**: This skill should be used at the start of any non-trivial task, whenever a request is ambiguous, and whenever deciding whether to ask the user a question or proceed. It gives the procedure for working out what a request is actually asking for before doing anything. - **read-the-research**: Answer questions from QuanterLab's published research studies and link to them. - **read-the-system**: Read the actual implementation before you state, design against, or test how any code you did not just write behaves, especially legacy, inherited, or AI-generated code. A name, type, signature, schema, or grep hit is a lead, not a fact; in these codebases they routinely lie, so open the file and trace the real path before concluding. Fires whenever you are about to claim 'this function does X', 'this parameter means Y', or 'this path is dead or live', and whenever a user asks how existing code actually works or whether it does what its name says. ([AlexTavor/engineering-discipline](https://github.com/AlexTavor/engineering-discipline/tree/HEAD/skills/read-the-system))
- **read-the-system**: Before asserting how unfamiliar code behaves at runtime — legacy, inherited, or AI-generated — open and read the actual implementation. A name, type, signature, schema, or grep hit is a lead, not a fact; in this kind of codebase they routinely lie. Use at the start of MAP, or any time you are about to make a load-bearing claim about behavior you did not just write.
